## 103. [DeltaTTG: Training Multi-Turn Agents with a Token-to-Go Progress Meter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17666) <a id="link103"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17666
**Authors:** Xuyan Ye, Xiyan Fu, Shengda Fan, Jiawei Cui, Yankai Lin, Wei Lu

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.17666v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) for multi-turn agents relies on sparse terminal rewards that say little about individual steps. Within-group step comparisons depend on reliable state matching and sufficiently diverse sampled outcomes, while direct step scoring may require costly external evaluators, human annotations, or task-specific heuristics. We introduce DeltaTTG to learn a reusable progress signal through a shared progress meter, using low-cost supervision from diverse policies' recorded trajectories without task-specific progress rules. Its supervision target, Token-to-Go (TTG), the number of tokens remaining to be generated until task completion, serves as an observable proxy for remaining completion effort. During actor training, changes in the frozen meter's estimates redistribute outcome-derived advantages across steps without altering the reward, preserving each trajectory's advantage sign and token-average scale. On ALFWorld, PaperSearchQA, and BFCLv3, DeltaTTG improves the average score over GRPO by 5.7 and 7.2 points with Qwen3-4B and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, respectively, outperforms all compared RL baselines on average, and keeps actor-training GPU-hours close to GRPO. Further experiments show improved sample efficiency and out-of-domain performance, and support the meter's ability to distinguish step quality and guide test-time action selection across actor sizes. The code is available at https://github.com/Xuyan923r/DeltaTTG.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 1 closely. Proposes a new training method (DeltaTTG) for multi-turn agents that improves instruction-following by learning a token-to-go progress meter, a specific fine-tuning step to improve instruction-following across tasks.
**Relevance:** 8
**Novelty:** 7

## 104. [Monitoring latent reasoning in looped language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18127) <a id="link104"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18127
**Authors:** Pawel Olszowiec, Paulina Tomaszewska, Maciej \.Zelaszczyk, Michal Byra, Neo Christopher Chung

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.18127v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Looped language models (LMs) reuse one block of layers several times per token generation, promising more latent computation at no additional storage cost. Despite their rising popularity, the analysis of looped LMs is still in its infancy: little is known about whether the iterative transformations actually improve their capabilities, or whether these models remain stable under prompt perturbation. We construct an inspection protocol rooted in Mechanistic Interpretability comprising five diverse lenses. We apply it to examine eight looped LMs from four families. Models differ in many aspects but the key one is whether the prompt is transmitted only within the looped state (carry loops: Ouro, LoopUS) or re-supplied at every iteration (re-read loops: Parcae, and Rec-OLMo & Huginn from the Raven family). The results of our study provide valuable insights concerning safety and efficient use of looped language models.

**Comment:** This paper presents a mechanistic interpretability study of looped language models, analyzing their internal iterative computations and stability under prompt perturbations. This matches criterion 8 on mechanistic interpretability techniques.
**Relevance:** 8
**Novelty:** 7

## 105. [SHELDON: Reasoning through Dual Search over Hypothesis and Observation Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18025) <a id="link105"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18025
**Authors:** Harshit Joshi, Priyank Shethia, Monica S. Lam

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.18025v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Test-time scaling has enabled LLMs to solve increasingly complex reasoning, discovery, and optimization tasks. However, existing approaches often gather observations while seeking evidence for current hypotheses, then generate new hypotheses conditioned on those hypotheses and their associated evidence. This couples observation discovery to existing hypotheses, favoring local refinements over exploration of unknown hypotheses regions. Inspired by the hypothetico-deductive method (Hempel, 1945) and dual-space models of scientific discovery (Klahr & Dunbar, 1988), we introduce SHELDON, a scientific reasoning harness that separately represents and jointly explores hypothesis and observation spaces. By actively seeking new observations along with new hypotheses, SHELDON can discover observations that its current hypotheses do not explain. These unexplained observations expose gaps in its understanding, motivate new investigations, and guide the generation of hypotheses beyond refinements of existing ones. Across diagnosis, abstract reasoning, and optimization benchmarks, SHELDON outperforms evaluated baselines by up to 26.7 points. Additional analysis shows that SHELDON generates up to 4.5x more novel solutions.

**Comment:** Author match

---

## 106. [Making Language the Low-Rate Code of Vision: Successive Refinement from Words to Pixels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16552) <a id="link106"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16552
**Authors:** Ruilin Gong, Hao Gong

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.16552v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: An ordered visual code lets a short prefix stand in for an image, and the training loss decides what that prefix contains. Under pixel reconstruction the first bits go to the directions of highest variance, and we prove that a caption can then be independent of the entire first half of a code that is rate-optimal for pixels at every length. In the rate-distortion sense a caption is therefore not the low-rate code of its image, unless the code is built to make it one. We study that construction and what it costs. We cast captioning and text-to-image generation as successive refinement under a distortion measure that changes along the code, from caption log-loss in the early stages to pixel distortion in the last, so that each stage is compared with its own optimum. Putting words first then costs nothing among the caption stages exactly when the captions form a summarization chain, and it costs the pixels at most the caption uncertainty left in a pixel-optimal reconstruction, which is large for short prefixes and small at full length. We build RUNG, an ordered code whose prefixes are supervised by a summarization ladder of captions and whose full length reconstructs the image; generation extends the code past the prompt and captioning truncates it. With data and capacity held fixed, ordering by language instead of reconstruction raises prefix-caption information 2.9 times and GenEval from 0.76 to 0.83, at a measured cost of 2.8 dB PSNR at a quarter of the code and 0.1 dB at full length. RUNG-XL (3.4B) reaches 0.88 GenEval and 87.6 DPG-Bench in 32 network evaluations and 0.91 GenEval when errors are redrawn at the rung where they appear; its coarse and fine captions agree 94.1% of the time, and it edits images without inversion.

**Comment:** Relevant to criterion 3: advances in diffusion language models. The paper studies an ordered visual code with a successive refinement approach from words to pixels, involving a generative model that integrates language and image generation with a novel coding scheme. It explicitly discusses generation and captioning as successive refinement, which is a novel generative modeling paradigm related to diffusion-like continuous refinement in language and vision.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 107. [ANTE: Anticipate, Then Execute -- Learning Internal World Models for Coding Agents from Verified Beliefs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15368) <a id="link107"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15368
**Authors:** Yi Ge, Kangrui Ruan, Jifan Chen, Manish Prajapat, Christian Bock, Xiaoyang Wang, Rein Houthooft, Aritra Sengupta, Varun Kumar

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.15368v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Rapid advances in large language models have made software engineering agents capable of tackling complex coding tasks. Within a containerized environment, a coding agent receives tool responses or execution results, and determines its later actions by those earlier results. However, most existing coding agents never look ahead: their policies do not anticipate the consequences of actions, and the terminal reward barely attributes a failure to certain earlier steps, so an ill-judged edit can corrupt the repository state irreversibly before any signal arrives. Recent code world models aim to address these challenges, but they are either trained and then frozen before reinforcement learning (RL), or reduced to an auxiliary next-token loss on the environment's outputs. We present ANTE (Anticipate, Then Execute), an agentic RL framework that verifies the policy's pre-action predictions by real execution and turns them into per-step credit for its actions and trust-or-abstain decisions. With an internal belief stated before each action, Belief-Anchored Policy Optimization (BAPO) turns the settled predictions into per-step advantages by anchoring credit on the policy's own forecasts. To turn the trained forecasts into better actions at inference, thereby facilitating effective test-time scaling, ANTE-TTO (test-time optimization) selects among candidate actions without an external scorer. ANTE raises Qwen3-32B from 25.0% to 57.2% pass@1 on SWE-bench Verified, 6.2% above a matched GRPO baseline trained without the belief state; its world model's accuracy climbs from 44% to 69% during RL, and the learned foresight capability transfers across harnesses and out-of-distribution domains. In summary, we aim to build a code world model that knows what has happened, what holds now, and what the next action will achieve.

**Comment:** ANTE presents an agentic RL framework for coding agents that anticipates consequences of actions and uses belief-anchored policy optimization. This is a novel RL method improving policy learning with internal world models and per-step credit assignment, related to criterion 1 (methodological improvements to RLHF or instruction-following fine-tuning steps) but applied to coding agents rather than general instruction-following LLMs.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

## 108. [Intent-Triggered Anchoring for Multi-Token Format Units in Parallel Decoding of Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16516) <a id="link108"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16516
**Authors:** Junhyeok Lee, Kyu Sung Choi

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.16516v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: In parallel decoding, diffusion language models commit several tokens per forward pass, so co-committed tokens cannot see each other, breaking multi-token format units that parsers need. On ECHO, an open-weight one-step chest X-ray (CXR) report generator, 32.7-36.7% of evaluation reports lack a parseable Impression heading and score as empty. We propose intent-triggered anchoring, a training-free decoding rule that completes a format unit once the model commits its first token, inserting only units with a unique, content-free completion; fallbacks write the unit when the model stops or loops without starting it. On three CXR evaluation sets, the rule raises readable rates from 63.3-67.3% to 100.0% with no regressions and a 95th-percentile latency ratio of 0.68-1.28 against official decoding. Broken headings state findings absent from the reference; adding the heading at the end leaves them in 15.5-31.0% of reports, our rule in at most 0.6%. For math answer markers, the rule raises accuracy in the most parallel setting of five diffusion language models by 5.8-20.8 points on GSM8K and 5.1-17.1 on GSM-Symbolic, held out from tuning. With lenient answer extraction, gains are 3.3-12.5 and 2.7-6.3 points; the rule adds 1.4-8.6 points over a stop-only fallback for four of the five models.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 3: Advances in diffusion language models. The paper proposes intent-triggered anchoring, a decoding rule to improve multi-token format units in parallel decoding of diffusion LMs, improving parseability and accuracy on benchmarks.
**Relevance:** 8
**Novelty:** 6

## 109. [Soft Teacher Forcing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17189) <a id="link109"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17189
**Authors:** Mohammad Fahes, Cordelia Schmid, Anurag Arnab

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.17189v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Soft tokens have so far been used to replace discrete chain-of-thought in latent reasoning methods. These approaches typically require multi-stage training and, in some cases, auxiliary modules, yet they still underperform supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on  triples. In this paper, we ask whether soft tokens can be used to improve SFT itself, instead of as an avenue for latent reasoning. We introduce Soft Teacher Forcing (Soft-TF), a simple training algorithm that combines teacher-forced embeddings with soft tokens through random convex combinations. The soft tokens are computed in a single parallel forward pass over the ground-truth sequence. Soft-TF adds no parameters, no external model and no curriculum, and leaves inference unchanged. Experiments show that Soft-TF improves over SFT on GSM8K, MATH, MathQA and AQuA, by +2.1 to +4.1 points on GSM8K across seven Gemma, Llama and Mistral models.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 1: proposes Soft Teacher Forcing, a novel training algorithm improving supervised fine-tuning (SFT) for instruction-following and reasoning tasks, improving over standard SFT without extra parameters or curriculum. Relevant to instruction-following fine-tuning improvements.
**Relevance:** 8
**Novelty:** 6

## 110. [Coupled Harness--Model Evolution for Scientific Equation Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17740) <a id="link110"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17740
**Authors:** Pengze Li, Jiaquan Zhang, Encheng Su, Yunbo Long, Zihang Zeng, Xinping Liu, Han Zhang, Junchi Yu, Shixiang Tang, Aoran Wang, Xi Chen

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.17740v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to discover scientific equations from experimental data. Such a discovery system has two parts that must work together: a library of executable computational tools and the model's tool-use policy. The tools supply the solving capability, from diagnosing structure in the data to fitting candidate equations; the policy plans and orchestrates their use. Yet existing systems adapt only one part, keeping either the toolset or the model fixed, which leaves the corresponding failures uncorrectable. We develop a coupled evolution framework that improves both parts from the same verifiable fit-error signal. In the tool-evolution phase, an agent grows an executable tool library from a small set of generic numerical operations by diagnosing its own failures: it authors a new tool when a capability is missing and revises its selection rules when the right tool went unused. In the policy-evolution phase, a small open model (roughly 3B active parameters) is fine-tuned on successful tool-use trajectories collected with the frozen library, on training formulas screened to be disjoint from all evaluation benchmarks. Ablations show the two phases are complementary: with no tool library at all the same model solves almost nothing, the tools account for most of the gain, and trajectory training adds a further gain that is largest on the most difficult problems. The full system solves 79.1/71.3\% of LSR-Synth problems at fit tolerances $10^{-2}$/$10^{-3}$, reaches 63.8\% pooled accuracy on the distributionally different SRSD-Feynman panel, and obtains a normalized CP3-28 aggregate of $-7.27$. These results suggest a broader recipe for scientific agents: let the model and its instruments improve each other under verifiable feedback.

**Comment:** This paper presents a coupled evolution framework jointly improving a model's tool-use policy and its executable tool library for scientific equation discovery. It involves fine-tuning a small open model on tool-use trajectories and improving instruction-following behavior via verifiable feedback. This matches criterion 1 (methodological improvements to instruction-following/fine-tuning).
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

## 111. [Self-Distillation with Verbal Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16354) <a id="link111"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16354
**Authors:** Jiazheng Li, Yawei Li, Junru Lu, Zhenyi Shen, Di Yin, Lin Gui, Xing Sun, David R\"ugamer, Xinpeng Wang, Yulan He

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.16354v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) trains a large language model using supervision from a teacher instantiated from the same model as the student. The teacher is conditioned on privileged information (PI), such as verified solutions. The student, which does not observe PI, learns from the teacher's next-token distributions along its own sampled responses. However, PI can bias supervision toward shortcut reasoning with limited verification or reconsideration, accompanied by a sharp drop in the entropy of the output distribution. To address this challenge, we introduce self-distillation with verbal regularization (VRSD), which uses system prompts to regulate how the teacher uses PI, thereby encouraging rethinking and exploration to counteract premature commitment. Theoretically, we model prompting as attenuating the influence of PI on the teacher distribution and derive conditions for entropy preservation. Across three models and four tasks, VRSD improves Pass@1 over OPSD in 11 of 12 model-task settings, by 3.9 percentage points on average, while generally maintaining higher token entropy.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 1: Proposes a new self-distillation method with verbal regularization to improve instruction-following and reasoning in language models, addressing entropy preservation and improving Pass@1 scores. This is a specific fine-tuning step to improve instruction-following.
**Relevance:** 8
**Novelty:** 6

## 112. [ProbabilityBench: Model Evaluation and Calibration with Exact Ground-Truth Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17016) <a id="link112"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17016
**Authors:** Yuyang Dai, Yuting Tang, Futoshi Futami, Takashi Ishida

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.17016v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Evaluating model uncertainty on real-world data is challenging because each instance provides only a single observed label, leaving true conditional class probabilities unknown. To address this, we introduce ProbabilityBench, a benchmark that provides exact, instance-level class probabilities across three simulated text-based domains: peer review, covert information transfer, and credit risk. By explicitly defining the full data-generating process for each environment, we analytically compute both exact class probabilities and Bayes error. Furthermore, we provide a theoretical guarantee that rendering these cases into natural-language memos strictly preserves their underlying generative probabilities. This exactness enables direct measurement of reported probability quality via KL divergence. Across 16 evaluated models, most models on the observed cost-quality Pareto frontiers are decision models, including Jev and Perplexity Decider. Finally, ProbabilityBench serves as a meta-evaluation framework, leveraging known ground-truth values to benchmark the accuracy of standard calibration metrics and Bayes-error estimators themselves.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 4: introduces ProbabilityBench, a benchmark with exact ground-truth uncertainty for model evaluation and calibration, enabling new paradigms for evaluating open-ended model uncertainty and calibration metrics.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

## 113. [On the Statistical Mechanics of Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17347) <a id="link113"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17347
**Authors:** Jerry Yao-Chieh Hu, Yu-An Chen, Maojiang Su, Han Liu

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.17347v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: We revisit the statistical mechanics of Transformer attention via modern Hopfield models. We present a free-energy formalism to characterize parameter regimes and critical thresholds for robust attention inference. The resulting free-energy landscape incorporates randomness in query states and attention weights. Attention then emerges as the equation of motion of the corresponding statistical-mechanical system (i.e., modern Hopfield model) through the standard free-energy minimization principle. This allows us to identify first-order phase transitions in attention between glassy and robust-inference phases. We further extend to attentions with stochastic queries and keys. Together, our results provide a rigorous statistical-mechanics framework for characterizing phase structure and inference reliability across a family of Transformer attention mechanisms.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 6: Provides a rigorous statistical mechanics framework for Transformer attention, characterizing phase transitions and inference reliability, which relates to theoretical understanding of scaling and model behavior.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

## 114. [Beyond Outcome Feedback: Trajectory-Quality-Guided Experience-based Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14877) <a id="link114"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14877
**Authors:** Bingjie Zhang, Yibo Yang, Qiantong Liu, Benshuo Fu, Yi Chang, Dandan Guo

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.14877v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Experience-based learning enables large language model (LLM) agents to improve over time by reusing knowledge from past executions. However, existing methods typically rely on final task outcomes as feedback for experience learning, overlooking the execution process and limiting their ability to evaluate and optimize experience based on trajectory quality. To address this limitation, we propose TQG-Exp, a trajectory-quality-guided experience-based learning framework that introduces process feedback. Specifically, we introduce Trajectory Quality Loss (TQL), which jointly considers task completion and process quality to provide a learning signal beyond final task outcomes. Based on this signal, we employ paired rollouts with and without retrieved experience to guide experience optimization. Representative trajectories are selected based on their outcomes and trajectory quality to extract task-level and task-type-level experiences. Meanwhile, differences in trajectory quality between the paired groups are used to estimate the contribution of retrieved experience and update its utility. The learned utility is then combined with semantic relevance to guide subsequent experience retrieval. Experiments on ALFWorld, WebShop, and ScienceWorld with two backbone LLMs show that TQG-Exp outperforms existing experience-based methods, improving Avg.@3 over the strongest baseline by 4.50 and 8.55 percentage points on DeepSeek-V4-Flash and Qwen3.5-Flash, respectively.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 1: Proposes a new fine-tuning framework (TQG-Exp) that improves instruction-following by incorporating trajectory-quality-guided experience learning beyond outcome feedback, which is a specific methodological improvement to instruction-following in LLMs.
**Relevance:** 8
**Novelty:** 6

## 115. [Hybrid Reasoning Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18861) <a id="link115"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18861
**Authors:** Jiaru Zou, Yunzhe Qi, Sirui Chen, Federico Bianchi, Shang Zhu, Pan Lu, Jingrui He, James Zou

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.18861v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: How can we build an agent that thinks beyond human language, yet still acts through it in the external environment? Modern LLM agents still reason and interact through text, while latent reasoning offers an efficient internal medium but lacks native tool access and reasoning transparency. We bridge these two directions with Hybrid Reasoning Agents (HybridAgents), which dynamically navigate between latent reasoning and text-based interaction throughout a unified generation process. Architecturally, a lightweight HybridLink enables seamless transitions between text and latent modes, while the agent's continuous working memory preserves lossless hybrid context across multiple turns. These designs preserve tool-integrated agentic capabilities while making latent thoughts directly inspectable. Building on this architecture, we further develop dedicated post-training algorithms for hybrid reasoning: HybridSFT stabilizes tool-integrated hybrid generation by teaching the backbone to coordinate with HybridLink, then HybridRL scales reasoning by learning when to think in latent space and when to act through text. We evaluate HybridAgent at 3 model scales across 11 benchmarks covering mathematics, science, medicine, search, and agentic reasoning. Compared with frontier LLM Agents and agentic frameworks spanning both text-based and latent methods, HybridAgent consistently improves performance by 10.4%, while delivering 35.5%-54.7% token reduction, 1.8x-2.6x faster inference, and 2.7x-3.7x cost savings. Code and data are available at https://github.com/togethercomputer/HybridAgent.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 12: advances in LLM reasoning capabilities and chain-of-thought reasoning by proposing Hybrid Reasoning Agents that combine latent reasoning and text-based interaction with new post-training algorithms including HybridRL.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

## 116. [What Can Language Models Inherit from Solomonoff Induction? Limits, Attainable Properties, and What Served Conditionals Reveal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14007) <a id="link116"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14007
**Authors:** Luan Ozelim, Abicumaran Uthamacumaran, Hector Zenil

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.14007v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: A familiar line of reasoning runs as follows: language models are flexible approximators trained under a strictly proper loss, so scale brings them closer to Solomonoff induction, and their predictive success is evidence of that approach. We apply known results from algorithmic information theory, universal prediction and Bayesian consistency. In its uniform form the conclusion holds for no computable model: each has cheap environments it never learns, and every uniformly computable family of total predictors shares one on which each member fails beyond a horizon growing with its index, so ``closer'' can hold only pointwise, one environment at a time. Nor could the offered routes certify even that: finite evaluation cannot certify agreement on rarely reached contexts, perfect corpus fit selects the corpus law as prior, and asymptotic criteria cannot see the constants that carry Solomonoff's guarantee. Our lens is the Kraft inequality read for predictors. Every model has at most the same budget of predictive success, so Solomonoff's predictor, which leaves part of it unspent, differs from a language model not in having more of it but in where it spends it; on mutually singular families this is exact, and only full-mass Bayes mixtures reach the frontier. We then discuss what is inheritable, including in-context learning as amortised Bayesian inference; what survives within a context window; which Solomonoff-like properties a deployed system should have or avoid; and what served conditionals determine. A closing table states what common practices certify and what to report. Tests are developed in a companion paper.

**Comment:** This paper analyzes theoretical limits of language models relative to Solomonoff induction, discussing predictive success, Bayesian consistency, and in-context learning as amortized Bayesian inference. It provides a conceptual explanation related to scaling and predictive properties of LMs, touching on fundamental theoretical aspects (criterion 6).
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 117. [Efficient Resource-Shiftable RL Post-training with Generative Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16866) <a id="link117"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16866
**Authors:** Yanfeng Zhao, Haoyang Li, Sheng Lin, Yuming Zhou, Jiawei Jiang, Bin Cui, Fangcheng Fu

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.16866v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) post-training with generative reward models (GenRM) has become a central recipe for improving large language models (LLMs), and it is also increasingly resource-intensive: policy rollout, GenRM-based reward evaluation, and policy training all compete for GPU resources. However, existing colocated and disaggregated systems rely on static resource allocation for these roles. Such allocation cannot adapt as each role's workload changes during RL and therefore leaves a role's resources underutilized.   We present PivotRL, an RL post-training system that dynamically shifts resources among different roles according to their runtime demands. PivotRL introduces a resource-shiftable runtime built on a Global Parameter Storage, together with a unified mechanism that efficiently shifts role-specific context. A shift-aware scheduler enables sequence migration upon a shift, while a planner considers the aggregate throughput benefit and the shift overhead to determine when and how to shift. We evaluate PivotRL on a 128-GPU cluster using four model groups spanning dense and mixture-of-experts models. PivotRL enables resource shifting within seconds and improves end-to-end throughput by 1.81-2.05 times over colocated deployment and 1.50-1.95 times over disaggregated deployment, while preserving RL convergence.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 1: Presents a new system (PivotRL) for efficient resource-shiftable RL post-training with generative rewards, directly related to RLHF and instruction-following fine-tuning improvements.
**Relevance:** 8
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 118. [Unbalanced W-Flow: Unbalanced Wasserstein Drifting under Finite-Batch Marginal Fluctuations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18552) <a id="link118"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18552
**Authors:** Jongwook Jeon, Mingue Park, Jiwon Choi, Jaihoon Kim, Yunhong Min, Minhyuk Sung

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.18552v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Teacher-free one-step generative models such as W-Flow learn directly from distributional objectives, with the Sinkhorn divergence providing the strongest empirical performance among the energies studied in W-Flow. However, Sinkhorn divergence is based on balanced optimal transport, whose exact marginal constraints can induce undesirable transport under finite minibatch fluctuations. We introduce UW-Flow, which replaces the balanced Sinkhorn energy with a debiased unbalanced Sinkhorn divergence and derives the corresponding Wasserstein velocity. The resulting dynamics retain W-Flow's cross-minus-self structure, recover the balanced limit, and preserve target identifiability under suitable assumptions. We further characterize the finite-batch velocity error of balanced and unbalanced transport by comparing each minibatch field with its population counterpart. Our analysis identifies conditions under which marginal relaxation yields smaller minibatch-to-population velocity error, and shows that for separated matched modes, balanced sensitivity grows with inter-mode separation while the corresponding Unbalanced Optimal Transport (UOT) sensitivity remains controlled by local transport geometry. Controlled experiments on fixed-support and continuous two-mode distributions support these predictions. On MNIST, CIFAR-10, and ImageNet-100, UW-Flow improves generation under matched training budgets, with the largest gains in unconditional generation and consistent improvements in the class-conditional setting.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 3: Advances in generative modeling with continuous diffusion models (W-Flow) for language-related distributions, introducing unbalanced Sinkhorn divergence to improve generation quality and stability under minibatch fluctuations.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 119. [Drift, Then Spread: Learning One-Step Generative Policies for Online Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15662) <a id="link119"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15662
**Authors:** Chunyu Luo, Hongyu Zhao, Guangyi Ni, Yue Song, Lingkai Kong

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.15662v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Generative policies offer expressive action distributions for online reinforcement learning (RL), but many diffusion- and flow-based policies require costly iterative sampling. We propose DriftSpread, a one-step generative policy based on Drifting for online maximum-entropy (MaxEnt) RL. To incorporate entropy without a tractable policy density, we build on an energy-based view of Drifting and derive critic-guided Drift and entropy-induced Spread from the negative MaxEnt objective. Our method learns these updates sequentially in the same actor, using action regression for value improvement and distribution matching to capture the effect of entropy. The actor updates require neither direct samples from the critic-defined Boltzmann target nor policy-density or score estimation. Under exact fitting with a fixed critic, we prove convergence to the Boltzmann target in the small-step limit followed by the long-time limit. On MuJoCo, DriftSpread achieves the highest aggregate normalized return among all evaluated methods, while outperforming the diffusion- and flow-based baselines in both training and inference efficiency.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 3: advances in diffusion language models applied to reinforcement learning policies, specifically a one-step generative policy based on drifting with theoretical convergence guarantees and improved efficiency over diffusion- and flow-based baselines.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 120. [Which Optimizer Leaks Most? Optimizer Implicit Bias as a Training-Data Privacy Controller](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18294) <a id="link120"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18294
**Authors:** Sagi Habani, Yehonathan Refael, Ofir Lindenbaum

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.18294v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Releasing a neural network checkpoint can leak information about its training data, and existing reconstruction results rely on gradient-descent training. We ask whether the choice of optimizer affects the ability to recover the training data. Extending work connecting Karush--Kuhn--Tucker conditions to training-data reconstruction, we show that the implicit biases of popular optimizers (SGD, Adam, Muon) leave very different fingerprints of the training data. We argue that the key distinction is the \emph{weight-specificity} of these implicit biases, namely how much continuous information about the final weights the optimizer-dependent target of the stationarity conditions encodes. The limiting stationarity conditions for models trained with SGD and Muon retain enough weight-specific information to reconstruct training examples in practice. For Adam in the $\ell_\infty$ implicit-bias regime, the optimizer-dependent part of these conditions encodes only a discrete pattern of maximal coordinates and their signs, and at nondegenerate KKT points the conditions are too few to identify the training set to first order. Experiments on MNIST and CIFAR-10 support these claims: models trained with Muon can yield stronger reconstructions at much lower training cost than models trained with SGD, although this advantage depends on the training regime and architecture, and models trained with Adam appear consistently more resistant to the evaluated attacks.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 2: Studies membership inference and training data reconstruction attacks related to optimizer implicit bias, providing new insights into training-data privacy leakage in neural networks, including language models.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 121. [Of Errors and Echoes: Responsibility-Aware Credit Assignment for Tool-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16027) <a id="link121"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16027
**Authors:** Xingjian Diao, Tianyu Yang, Wenjun Huang, Xiangchi Yuan, Chunhui Zhang, Xingchen Zhao, Weiyi Wu, Haotian Xu, Soroush Vosoughi, Xiangliang Zhang, Jiang Gui

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.16027v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Reinforcement learning improves tool-use agents, yet fine-grained credit assignment remains challenging even with step-level feedback. An observed error may be inherited from an earlier decision, while multiple responsible decisions may share the same downstream consequence or participate in later recovery. We introduce Error-Chain-Aware Optimization (ECHO), a responsibility-aware credit assignment framework that separates responsibility identification from credit allocation. A frozen judge combines retrospective trajectory analysis with decision-time assessment from the policy-visible prefix to distinguish newly introduced errors, inherited consequences, missed corrections, and successful corrections. ECHO then counts shared downstream consequences once across responsible decisions and constrains rewards for repairing self-induced errors through source-funded recovery, producing bounded action-level supervision alongside terminal supervision. Experiments on six diverse tool-use benchmarks at both 4B and 8B scales show strong and consistent improvements over the compared baselines, with ECHO achieving the highest score on five of six benchmarks at each scale. These results support decision responsibility as a useful basis for fine-grained credit assignment in tool-use agents. Our code is available at https://github.com/xid32/ECHO.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 1: Introduces Error-Chain-Aware Optimization (ECHO), a responsibility-aware credit assignment method for reinforcement learning agents, improving fine-grained credit assignment which is relevant to instruction-following improvements in RLHF contexts.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 122. [RIVO: Lightweight Adaptive Rerouting for Deep Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15910) <a id="link122"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15910
**Authors:** Mingchen Li, Jiatan Huang, Hong Yu

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.15910v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Deep Research agents rely on long-horizon reasoning and iterative tool use to solve complex information-seeking tasks. However, adaptive decision-making over these trajectories remains challenging, as agents may stop prematurely when further evidence is needed, continue unnecessarily after sufficient evidence has been gathered, or fail to redirect unproductive trajectories. Existing solutions either require costly model retraining and dense turn-level supervision, or rely on heuristic control rules that are not directly optimized for downstream research utility. This motivates a lightweight, utility-aware alternative for Deep Research control. We formulate adaptive rerouting in long-horizon Deep Research as a state-conditioned trajectory correction problem. Based on this view, we introduce RIVO, a lightweight plug-in framework for adaptive trajectory correction. The agent estimates its research state from lightweight signals and determines whether trajectory redirection is needed. Once triggered, the plug-in modifies the Host token logits to steer generation toward a more promising research direction. Theoretical analysis shows that, under a value-alignment condition and bounded local curvature, a sufficiently small logit intervention can locally improve expected downstream research utility. We further construct BrowseCore, a curated dataset with a paired local corpus, for efficient and controlled Deep Research experimentation. Building on this resource, the plug-in is trained from downstream outcomes, enabling it to learn corrections that improve subsequent research and final task performance. Extensive experiments across multiple Deep Research benchmarks and control settings demonstrate the effectiveness of our method, showing that lightweight logit intervention can improve long-horizon research without retraining the underlying Host model.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 13 (AI control, oversight, or monitoring of AI system behavior during deployment). The paper proposes RIVO, a lightweight plug-in framework for adaptive trajectory correction in deep research agents, which involves runtime control and monitoring of generation trajectories without retraining the base model.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 123. [From Stages to States: Unified Iterative Reasoning for MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15620) <a id="link123"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15620
**Authors:** Chuang Yu, Xuefei Ma, Yuanhao Feng, Wuxiong Zeng, Bo Wang, Minghong Cai, Dongming Wu, Fei Zhou, Chen Tang, Wenwen Tong, Lewei Lu, Xiangyu Yue

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.15620v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Multimodal Chain-of-Thought (MCoT) substantially enhances the complex reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) by explicitly modeling intermediate reasoning processes. However, most learning-based MCoT methods follow a stage-centric paradigm, where different reasoning behaviors are assigned to predefined stages and optimized separately. This results in fragmented reasoning and a training-inference reasoning-state gap. To address these limitations, we propose ReState, a unified iterative reasoning framework that reformulates multimodal reasoning from a stage-driven pipeline into a state-driven dynamic process. It comprises two complementary components: Unified Reasoning State Modeling (URSM) and Closed-Loop Reasoning-State Learning (CRSL). URSM unifies reasoning construction, correct reasoning preservation, and erroneous reasoning correction as reasoning-state transitions within a shared state space, enabling these heterogeneous reasoning behaviors to be learned jointly. CRSL further incorporates self-generated historical reasoning states during training and recursively updates the current state during inference, thereby mitigating the reasoning-state gap and enabling iterative self-refinement without requiring an additional reflection module, critic, or independent correction stage. Furthermore, we construct VersaCoT-10K, a high-quality CoT dataset spanning eight visual reasoning domains, which provides diverse supervision for multi-domain reasoning and reasoning-state refinement. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ReState exhibits strong adaptability across different MLLM backbones and achieves strong performance on multiple public reasoning benchmarks, supporting the shift from stage-centric reasoning toward state-centric reasoning. Our data and code are available at https://github.com/YuChuang1205/ReState

**Comment:** Matches criterion 12: proposes a unified iterative reasoning framework (ReState) for multimodal LLMs that models reasoning as state transitions and enables iterative self-refinement, improving reasoning capabilities and addressing reasoning-state gaps.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 124. [Structural Chain-of-Thought: Reasoning with Executable Computational Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15107) <a id="link124"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15107
**Authors:** Petr Senatorov, Karim Vafin, Elena Tutubalina, Oleg Somov

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.15107v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Chain-of-thought prompting enables reasoning in large language models but produces explanations that are difficult to verify, expensive to generate, and not always faithful. We introduce a Structural Chain-of-Thought framework where models generate executable computational graphs instead of free-form reasoning. The final answer is obtained by deterministically executing the graph. The model is trained using graph-based reinforcement learning that rewards structural validity, answer correctness, and node overlap with reference graphs.   Experiments across five benchmarks and four model families show that our method consistently improves accuracy over supervised graph training and can reduce generation length by up to 5-6x compared with standard GRPO and chain-of-thought supervised training. Our faithfulness experiments reveal that models trained with our approach tend to rely on deriving their answers from the generated graphs rather than producing post-hoc rationalizations as usually in chain-of-thought reasoning.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 12: introduces Structural Chain-of-Thought with executable computational graphs and graph-based RL to improve reasoning faithfulness and efficiency in LLMs, advancing chain-of-thought reasoning capabilities.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 125. [Radioactive Feedback: Tracing Models Trained by Label-Only LLM Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15685) <a id="link125"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15685
**Authors:** Yuejia Li, JunHeng Li, Hao Wu, Ke He, Mang Ye

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.15685v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Conventional distillation transfers capability through teacher-generated demonstrations. LLM-as-a-Judge knowledge distillation offers a distinct and increasingly practical alternative. In the label-only variant studied here, the student generates every response and improves from scalar scores alone. Prior work shows that this non-demonstrative feedback can improve student capability, but it creates a fundamentally different provenance problem. No teacher-generated answer enters the student's training set, so any evaluator-specific trace must pass through a low-bandwidth reward signal rather than copied text. Existing watermarks and distillation detectors do not cover this score-only channel. We introduce Radioactive Feedback, which encodes a registered pattern of behavioral preferences in the rewards themselves. The evaluator applies bounded pointwise adjustments to quality-qualified responses, allowing repeated optimization to transfer the pattern to a student trained by another party. Experiments on code and mathematics show that the registered preferences transfer through scores alone and across datasets, enabling black-box detection against a fixed reference pool.

**Comment:** Introduces Radioactive Feedback, a method to trace models trained by label-only LLM supervision via behavioral pattern encoding in reward signals. This relates to RLHF/instruction-following training methods and detection of training provenance, relevant to criterion 1 and possibly 2 (membership inference).
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 126. [Efficient Safety Guardrails through Latent Dual-Hypothesis Reasoning with On-Demand Trace Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18304) <a id="link126"></a>
**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18304
**Authors:** Md Asiful Islam, Fahmida Alam, Mihai Surdeanu

**Abstract:** arXiv:2610.18304v1 Announce Type: new  Abstract: Guardrail approaches that rely on reasoning-based large language models (LLM) improve safety decisions, but come with substantial inference overhead due to the explicit reasoning. We introduce LR-Guard (Latent Reasoning Guardrail), an efficient and explainable guardrail that jointly learns structured latent reasoning and on-demand reasoning reconstruction. Our method distills a frozen teacher's analysis of safe and unsafe prompt interpretations and their comparison into compact latent representations. Joint training optimizes these representations for both safety prediction and reconstruction of the teacher's explicit reasoning traces, enabling human-readable explanations without requiring intermediate text generation for every decision. Experiments show that LR-Guard outperforms its explicit-reasoning teacher in most in-domain and out-of-domain settings on safety classification and explanation extraction, while achieving a 19.4x inference speedup at batch size one. Further, reconstructed reasoning traces from the latent information exhibit high semantic similarity to the teacher's explicit reasoning.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 4: Proposes a new paradigm for efficient and explainable safety guardrails via latent dual-hypothesis reasoning and on-demand trace reconstruction, fundamentally rethinking open-ended evaluation and explanation of LLM safety decisions.
**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7
